# Ascend Now — Verified Live Database State

**Generated 2026-07-01 by direct introspection of the live Supabase project (`ascend-now`, ref `uqyuczvckqxgtilpzarh`, org `ascend-now`) via the Supabase MCP connection — every table, column, constraint, policy, function, and trigger below was read straight from the running Postgres instance, not from the migration files or any prior doc.**

**Updated 2026-09-12 (shared packages go to TWO NAMED children, and only for the course types sold that way — new `student_package_members` table, `course_types.is_shareable`, two validating triggers, membership-aware `student_package_ids()`/RLS/usage RPC).** Applied to the live project (ref `rpavogsdladesmghwodg`) via Supabase MCP `apply_migration` as `supabase/migrations/20260912000000_shared_package_members.sql`, verified live by impersonation.

Family packages as shipped on 2026-09-11 let **every** child of the owning parent spend the pool, for **any** course type. Two business rules change that: only Academic and Beyond Academic (where Passion Projects live) are sold as shared hours, and a shared package goes to exactly **two children an admin names** — the parent is never asked to choose, and a third sibling must not quietly start drawing on a pool sold for two.

So `parent_id` still says which household owns a pool (it is what groups it on the family's page and what `student_packages_one_current_per_family_course_type` keys on), but it no longer says who may spend it. **`student_package_members` does.**

**Schema:**
- `course_types.is_shareable boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false` — true for **Academic** and **Beyond Academic** only. A column rather than a name list in code, so selling a new programme as shared is one row edit; the default of false means a new course type is bought per student until someone says otherwise. Foundation Program / All-In-One stay false: every bundle contains a College Counselling pool.
- `student_package_members (student_package_id bigint → student_packages ON DELETE CASCADE, student_id text → students ON DELETE CASCADE, added_by_user_id uuid → auth.users, created_at timestamptz)`, PK `(student_package_id, student_id)`, plus `idx_student_package_members_student_id`. RLS enabled; `select/insert/update/delete` granted to `authenticated` and `service_role`.

**Backfilled** with every child currently linked to the owning parent, so nothing live changed hands: **12 rows** — both of `DEVG26-5`'s children (`BATO26-1`, `TESX26-5`) on all six of that family's pools.

**Two validating triggers** (both SECURITY DEFINER, `SET search_path = public`) — a CHECK constraint can't reach another table, so these are the enforcement:
- **`validate_student_package_member()`** on `student_package_members` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE) — the package must exist and be parent-owned, the student must be a child of that same parent, and a package already holding 2 other members refuses a third. **Change the 2 here and in `SHARED_PACKAGE_STUDENT_COUNT` (`src/utils/sharedPackages.ts`) together.**
- **`validate_shared_package_course_type()`** on `student_packages` (BEFORE INSERT OR **UPDATE OF `parent_id`, `course_type_id`**) — a parent-owned package's course type must be `is_shareable`. Scoping the UPDATE to those two columns is deliberate and load-bearing twice over: `recompute_package_hours_used()`'s `hours_used` writes never trip it, and the **pre-existing non-shareable family pools are grandfathered** rather than the migration guessing which single child should inherit `DEVG26-5`'s College Counselling and All-In-One pools. Those five legacy pools remain shared; the rule binds every package created from here on.

**`student_package_ids(p_student_id text)` rewritten** — a student's own packages `UNION` the shared packages they are a **named member of**. Being a child of the owning parent is no longer part of the definition, so the session-log router, RLS and the UI's fetch all narrow together.

**`can_view_package` / `is_my_assigned_package` re-signed to take the package id** (`(p_package_id bigint, p_student_id text, p_parent_id text)`), because membership is per package and the two-argument versions had no way to ask. `can_view_package` now admits the parent themselves **or a child named on that pool**; `is_my_assigned_package` reaches a shared pool through any **member** on the coach's roster. The 2026-09-11 two-argument versions were dropped after the five policies that used them (`student_parent_read_packages`, `pc_manage_assigned_packages` on `student_packages`; `student_parent_read_topups`, `pc_select_own_topups`, `pc_insert_own_topups` on `package_topups`) were recreated against the new ones.

**Three new policies on `student_package_members`:** `admin_all_package_members` (`is_admin()`), `student_parent_read_package_members` (membership rows follow their package's visibility — if you may see the pool, you may see who shares it) and `pc_manage_assigned_package_members`.

**`family_package_usage_by_student(p_package_id bigint)` widened** to return a row for **every named member**, spent or not (0 sessions / 0 hours), `UNION`ed with anyone who has actually drawn on the pool — so a legacy pool's usage is still fully attributed, and "shared by Batu and Test1" now reads off the same single call that says who spent what. The caller-can-see-the-package guard is unchanged.

**Verified live** (every write inside a rolled-back transaction; afterwards re-confirmed at 6 packages / 12 members / 88 session logs / 2 students):
- Guards, each rejected with its own message: a shared **College Counselling** package (`"College Counselling is bought per student and cannot be shared between siblings."`); a member on a **missing** package; a member on a **student-owned** package; a **third** member on a package that already has two (`"A shared package is shared by 2 students; package 17 already has 2."`); a duplicate member (the PK).
- Impersonating **Batu** (`set local role authenticated` + his `sub` claim) with membership intact: **6** packages, **12** member rows, and `family_package_usage_by_student(1)` returning `BATO26-1 = 86.75`, `TESX26-5 = 0.75`.
- The same impersonation with **his** member row on pool 1 deleted first: **5** packages, **10** member rows, **5** topups, and the usage RPC on pool 1 returning **no rows** — the pool, its top-ups, its membership and its usage all disappear together for a non-member.
- `student_package_ids('BATO26-1')` then excludes pool 1 while `student_package_ids('TESX26-5')` still includes it.
- **The router honours it too**: an Academic session logged for Batu with his membership removed did **not** deduct from the family's 100-hour Academic pool — it created a fresh **zero-hour, student-owned** fallback pool (`pool_fallback_used = true`, `pool_ambiguous = false`), exactly as it would for a student with no package at all. With membership intact the same session routes to pool 1 (`parent_id = DEVG26-5`, `pool_fallback_used = false`).

**Edge function redeployed:** `notify-package-threshold` (version 11, `verify_jwt` unchanged at true). It resolved the PC to email via `student_packages.student_id`, which is **NULL on every shared pool** — so since family packages shipped on 2026-09-11, a shared pool could run to 100% or go over limit and nobody was ever emailed (`.eq("student_id", null)` matched no assignment, and the function returned `skipped: "Student has no assigned PC"`). It now falls back to the session log's own `student_id` — the child who actually sat the session that crossed the threshold, whose coach is the one who can act on it.

**Updated 2026-09-11, later (data only — Batu Ozcelik's six packages moved to his family, his sibling's unused Academic pool deleted).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260911000100_move_batu_packages_to_family.sql`, verified live. No schema change.

Family packages shipped earlier the same day, but Batu's hours were bought long before his parent account existed, so all six pools were still student-owned and invisible to his sibling. The family is `DEVG26-5` (devender gupta) with two children: `BATO26-1` (Batu, 6 packages) and `TESX26-5` (Test1, 1 unused Academic package).

**All six of Batu's packages became parent-owned** — Academic 100h, the All-In-One bundle's four Beyond Academic pools, and College Counselling 40h. Usage carried over untouched: `hours_used` is computed from `session_logs`, which are keyed on `student_package_id` and not rewritten here, and each session still carries its own `student_id`, so the per-sibling breakdown attributes every one of those hours to Batu rather than to the family at large.

**Test1's own Academic pool was deleted**, and that half is not optional. Leaving it would have given Test1 two Academic candidates — their own and the newly-shared one — which `handle_session_log_package_lock()` cannot choose between: simulated before writing the migration, an Academic session for Test1 landed with `student_package_id = NULL, pool_ambiguous = true`, deducting from nothing and waiting in the PC's Pending Deduction queue. The pool had never been used (0 sessions, 0 hours). `package_topups` cascaded and `coordinator_log_package_statuses` set null on their own; `enrollment_request_packages.resulting_student_package_id` is the one FK here with no ON DELETE behaviour and was nulled first, leaving the enrollment request itself intact. The shared Academic pool therefore stays at its original **100h** rather than becoming 124h — a deliberate choice to drop those 24 purchased hours rather than merge them.

The migration is keyed on student ids rather than `student_packages.id`, and guards itself: it refuses to run if Batu has no parent, refuses to delete the sibling's pool if it has gained any session since, and asserts at the end that none of Batu's packages are still student-owned and that exactly one unlocked family Academic pool exists.

**Verified live afterwards:** an Academic session logged for *either* sibling now resolves to the shared pool (`pool_ambiguous = false`, `pool_fallback_used = false`). As an admin the split reads Batu 70 sessions/86.75h Academic, 3/1.25h Primary Project, 12/9.25h College Counselling. As **Test1**: 6 pools visible, **0** session logs, only their own `students` row, and the Academic split still showing "Batu 86.75h" — the shape point 8 of the family-package design requires. As **Batu**: the same 6 pools and 87 session logs, `count(distinct student_id) = 1`, all his own.

*(Note: `family_package_usage_by_student` returns no rows to a caller with no JWT — `is_admin()` and `can_view_package()` are both false for `auth.uid() = NULL` — so querying it over a service-role connection looks empty. That is the guard working, not a bug; check it as an impersonated user.)*

**Updated 2026-09-11 (family packages — a pool bought by a parent and drawn down by every sibling; `student_packages.parent_id`, nullable `student_id`, a widened session-log router, and a per-sibling usage RPC).** Applied to the live project (ref `rpavogsdladesmghwodg`) via Supabase MCP `apply_migration` as `supabase/migrations/20260911000000_family_packages.sql`, verified live end to end.

Hours were owned by a student, so a family that bought 100 Academic hours had to split them into two packages up front and whichever child ran out first was stuck while the other's balance sat unused. **A package is now owned by exactly one of a student or a parent** — `CHECK (num_nonnulls(student_id, parent_id) = 1)`, added `NOT VALID` then validated separately so it would fail loudly on any pre-existing violation rather than silently take a long lock. `student_id` is now nullable; every package that existed before this change is student-owned and completely unaffected.

**Schema:** `student_packages.parent_id text REFERENCES parents(id)` (partial index `idx_student_packages_parent_id`), and a new partial unique index `student_packages_one_current_per_family_course_type` on `(parent_id, course_type_id, coalesce(pool_label,'')) WHERE (not is_locked) and parent_id is not null` — the existing per-student index stops constraining family rows the moment `student_id` goes null, since Postgres treats NULLs as distinct in a unique index.

**What did NOT need changing, and why it matters:** `recompute_package_hours_used()` is untouched. It already sums `session_logs.session_duration_hrs` by `student_package_id` without caring whose student a row belongs to, so two siblings drawing on one pool sum correctly as-is. Per-sibling attribution likewise needed no new bookkeeping — `session_logs.student_id` already says which child sat the session.

**`handle_session_log_package_lock()`** is the one behavioural change: its candidate-count lookup, its sticky (teacher, subject) resolution and its `pool_label` direct match all widen from `student_id = NEW.student_id` to `id IN (SELECT student_package_ids(NEW.student_id))` — the student's own pools **plus** their family's. Every other branch (the locked-package guards, the keep-the-existing-assignment shortcut on UPDATE, the ambiguity flag) is byte-for-byte what it was. The zero-hour fallback pool it creates when no pool of the right course type exists is deliberately still **student**-owned: it exists to make an overage visible against the right course type, and inventing a family pool there would silently create shared hours nobody bought.

**New functions** (all SECURITY DEFINER, `SET search_path = public`, granted EXECUTE to `authenticated`/`service_role`):
- **`student_package_ids(p_student_id text) → setof bigint`** — every pool a student may draw on. One definition, used by the trigger, by RLS and by the UI's fetch, so "what can this student spend" cannot come to mean three different things.
- **`can_view_package(p_student_id text, p_parent_id text)`** — the read gate for a package whichever way it is owned: `can_view_student()` for a student-owned one; for a family one, the parent themselves **or any of their children** (the children are the ones spending it).
- **`is_my_assigned_package(p_student_id text, p_parent_id text)`** — the staff equivalent; a coach reaches a family pool through any of that family's children on their roster.
- **`family_package_usage_by_student(p_package_id bigint)`** — per-child `sessions`/`no_shows`/`hours` totals for one package, applying the same No Show 1/2 rule `recompute_package_hours_used()` does. It re-checks the caller can see the package before returning anything, and returns no rows rather than raising when they can't.

**Policies rewritten** onto the two ownership-aware helpers: `student_parent_read_packages` and `pc_manage_assigned_packages` on `student_packages`; `student_parent_read_topups`, `pc_select_own_topups` and `pc_insert_own_topups` on `package_topups`.

**Why the usage RPC exists at all** — this is the interesting constraint. A student must be able to see that their sibling used 12 of the family's hours **without** being able to read any of that sibling's session logs. RLS on `session_logs` rightly shows a student only their own rows, so no client-side GROUP BY could ever produce a sibling's total; the aggregate has to happen inside SECURITY DEFINER, where the total gets out and the detail stays in.

**Verified live** (every write inside rolled-back transactions; the database was confirmed back to 0 family packages and 0 probe auth users afterwards):
- Two siblings with no packages of their own, one 50-hour family Academic pool: **all three sessions from both children routed to that same pool**, `pool_fallback_used = false`, `pool_ambiguous = false`.
- `hours_used` came to **10.5** from 3 + 2 + 5.5 with a No Show 1 correctly costing nothing; remaining 39.5.
- Impersonating one sibling as `authenticated`: **1** package visible (the shared pool), **2** session logs visible and `count(distinct student_id) = 1` — only their own — the sibling's `students` row **not** readable, and `family_package_usage_by_student` nonetheless returning both children's totals (Elder 2 sessions/5 hrs, Younger 1 session/5.5 hrs).
- Full flow as the parent: 50 bought / 20 used, split Ava 12 hrs, Ben 8 hrs.

**Edge function:** new **`create-student-with-user`** (admin-only) — creates the Auth account, the `students` row and the PC assignment, and emails credentials, exactly the way `create-teacher-with-user` does. It deliberately writes no `enrollment_requests` row and involves no invoice, payment link or payment proof; the enrollment workflow (§2.9) stays for when money is actually changing hands.

**Updated 2026-09-10 (parent accounts return, as households — new `parents` table, `students.parent_id`, `enrollment_requests.parent_id`, the `parent` role, and read access for the parent dashboard).** Applied to the live project (ref `rpavogsdladesmghwodg`) via Supabase MCP `apply_migration` as `supabase/migrations/20260910000000_parent_role_enum.sql` and `20260910000100_parent_accounts_and_dashboard.sql`, verified live and RLS-tested by impersonation. *(Note: the "Generated 2026-07-01" header above names ref `uqyuczvckqxgtilpzarh`; the live project this was applied to — and introspected against, schema and data matching this repo exactly — is `rpavogsdladesmghwodg`. Worth reconciling that header.)*

The `parent` role and `parents` table were removed on 2026-07-07 (entry below) when the student and parent dashboards were merged. They are back for a **different** reason than the one they were dropped for: the old parent account duplicated one student's dashboard, whereas this one is a **household** — one login owning any number of students — which cannot be expressed as fields on a student row. The plain `parent_full_name`/`parent_phone_number` columns stay exactly as they are and are independent of the link.

Split into two migration files deliberately: Postgres will not let a value added by `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` be *used* in the same transaction that adds it, and every migration here runs inside one. Part 2 never writes the literal `'parent'` (its helpers test for a `parents` row, not for the role), so the pair applies cleanly in either order of tooling.

**New table — `parents`** (RLS enabled): `id text PK` (mnemonic, e.g. `SARK26-1` — 3 letters of first name + 1 of last + 2-digit year + `-` + `nextval('parents_num_seq')`, generated by the `trg_generate_parent_id` BEFORE INSERT trigger, which no-ops when an id is supplied), `first_name text NOT NULL`, `last_name text NOT NULL`, `email text`, `phone_number text`, `user_id uuid REFERENCES public.users(id)`, `is_active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true`, `created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`. Partial unique index `parents_user_id_key` on `user_id WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL` (one login → at most one parent, same guarantee `students.user_id`/`teachers.user_id` carry); partial index `parents_email_lower_idx` on `lower(email) WHERE email IS NOT NULL` backing the "is this family already here?" lookup. Grants: `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` to `authenticated`, `ALL` to `service_role`, plus explicit `USAGE` on `parents_num_seq` to both — the sequence backs a text PK through a trigger, not an identity column, so per `db/README.md`'s "one rule to never forget" the table INSERT grant alone is not enough.

**New columns:** `students.parent_id text REFERENCES public.parents(id)` (nullable, indexed `idx_students_parent_id`) — the household link; and `enrollment_requests.parent_id text REFERENCES public.parents(id)`, so the parent picked on the enroll form survives to the confirm step. Both carry `COMMENT ON COLUMN` explaining they are independent of the plain guardian contact fields.

**Helpers** (all SECURITY DEFINER, `SET search_path = public` — every cross-table test goes through one, so a policy on `students` never subqueries `parents` inline and cannot recreate the infinite recursion fixed on 2026-07-07):
- **`can_view_student(p_student_id text)`** — **widened**: true when the caller is that student **or a parent linked to them**. This is the whole access story in one change; the 13 existing policies that call it (on `students`, `student_packages`, `package_topups`, `session_logs`, `invoices`, `invoice_line_items`, `invoice_packages`, `generated_papers`, `grades`, `subject_notes`, `pc_student_assignments`, `cc_student_assignments`, `submissions`) were left untouched and now cover parents too. Carries a `COMMENT ON FUNCTION` stating it is a READ gate only.
- **`is_own_student_record(p_student_id text)`** — new, and byte-for-byte the **old** `can_view_student()` body ("the caller's own student row"). See the trap below.
- **`my_parent_id()`** — the caller's own `parents.id`, or null.
- **`is_my_linked_child(p_parent_id text)`** — restored from 2026-07-07; lets a policy test a `parent_id` column without subquerying `parents`.

**The trap this migration had to close.** Exactly one policy keyed on `can_view_student()` is FOR ALL rather than SELECT: `submissions`' **"Students manage their own submissions"**. Widening the helper would therefore have silently granted parents *write* access — a parent could have started, edited and submitted their child's homework. That policy is now keyed on `is_own_student_record()` instead, so student behaviour is **completely unchanged**, and a new SELECT-only **`parent_read_children_submissions`** gives parents the read half the dashboard needs ("handed in vs not"). Standing rule: **`can_view_student()` is a read gate; any policy granting writes must use `is_own_student_record()`.**

**New policies** (4 beyond the submissions split): on `parents` — `admin_all_parents` (ALL, `is_admin()`), `parent_read_own` (SELECT, `user_id = auth.uid()`; nothing lets a parent write their own row, matching how a student's details are admin-maintained), `staff_read_parents` (SELECT, scoped through `teachers` — deliberately **not** through `students`, which is what the 2026-07-05 `pc_read_assigned_parents` policy did and what caused the recursion; staff can already read every student row including the guardian name/phone, so this exposes nothing new). On `users` — `parents_read_children_users` (SELECT, each child's user row; expressed inline rather than via a helper because `users`' own policies never subquery `students`/`parents`, so there is no cycle to break — same reasoning as the 2026-07-08 version). On `pc_profiles` — `parents_read_children_coach_pc_profile` and `parents_read_children_counsellor_pc_profile`, the household twins of the two `Students read their assigned …` policies.

**Verified live by impersonation** (a real parent-only login — `auth.users` → trigger-created `public.users` with role `parent` → `parents` row → one of the two students linked — inside rolled-back transactions; the database was confirmed back to 0 `parents` rows, 0 linked students, 0 `parent` users and 0 probe auth users afterwards). Reading as that parent: **exactly one** of the two students visible (`BATO26-1`, not `TESX26-5`), 87 session logs all belonging to that single student (`count(distinct student_id) = 1`), 6 packages, 1 homework paper, 1 published report, 1 PC assignment, and 1 `parents` row (their own). Writing as that parent: `UPDATE students SET school` affected **0 rows** and `INSERT INTO submissions` was **refused** — both blocked, as intended. `teachers` stays readable (16 rows) via the long-standing "Any authenticated user can read teacher names" policy, which is what renders teacher names in the session-log list.

**Edge functions:** new **`create-parent-with-user`** (admin-only, `verify_jwt`) — creates the Auth user with `role: "parent"` in `user_metadata` (so `trg_create_user_profile` writes the `public.users` row), inserts the `parents` row, and emails credentials; username is the email local part de-duplicated against `users.username`, password is `<firstname>@ascendnow`, the same convention teacher and student accounts use. Rolls the Auth user back if the `parents` insert fails, and 409s on an email a family already uses. **`review-enrollment-payment`** redeployed (v11) with one change: the new-student insert now also carries `parent_id: enrollment.parent_id ?? null`. It never creates a parent login — by confirm time the account already exists.

**Updated 2026-08-03 (College Counsellors are now peers of Performance Coaches — every PC policy widened to cover a CC).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260808000000_cc_gets_pc_access.sql`, verified live and RLS-tested by impersonation. The CC was introduced (2026-07-31, below) as a deliberately narrow role — their own session logs, their own roster, nothing else. That decision is **reversed**: a counsellor now sees and does everything a coach does, including filing package renewal requests for their assigned students. This also supersedes `20260806000200_cc_reads_only_own_session_logs.sql`; a counsellor reads session logs exactly as a coach does again.

Rather than adding a second, near-identical policy beside every `is_performance_coach()` one, all **32** of them were rewritten in place — **same policy names**, so every reference to them elsewhere in this file still resolves — on top of two new helpers:

- **`is_coach_or_counselor()`** (SECURITY DEFINER, `SET search_path = 'public'`) — `is_performance_coach() or is_college_counselor()`. Note the two halves are keyed differently and deliberately so: PC off `users.role`, CC off the `teachers` flag (see the 2026-07-31 entry), which is what lets a PC-and-CC match on both.
- **`is_my_assigned_student(p_student_id text)`** (SECURITY DEFINER, `SET search_path = 'public'`) — true when the caller holds a **live** `pc_student_assignments` **or** `cc_student_assignments` row for that student. This is the half that actually makes a counsellor's access useful: the roster-scoped PC policies all joined `pc_student_assignments` directly, so a CC (who has no rows there) would have matched nothing even with the role test widened. Every such policy now calls this instead of inlining the join.

Both are granted `EXECUTE` to `authenticated`/`service_role`. **`set_student_status()`** was rewritten on the same two helpers, so a counsellor can move their own student between active/paused/completed exactly as a coach can. The one policy still keyed to `is_performance_coach()` alone is **`pc_read_own_assignments`** on `pc_student_assignments`, which is correct — a counsellor's own assignments live in `cc_student_assignments` under `cc_read_own_assignments`.

One genuinely new policy: **`Students read their assigned counsellor pc_profile`** on `pc_profiles` (SELECT, `authenticated`) — the CC twin of `Students read their assigned coach pc_profile`, joining through `cc_student_assignments` instead. `pc_profiles` is the single profile table for **both** roles; a counsellor's card is a row in it keyed by their `teacher_id`, written by an admin from the new `/admin/ccs/:id` → **Profile** tab the same way a coach's is written from `/admin/pcs/:id`. Policy count **149 → 150**; no table, column, constraint, trigger or index changed.

**Verified live by impersonation** (all writes inside rolled-back transactions — `pc_profiles` confirmed back to 5 rows and `package_renewal_requests` to 0 afterwards): counsellor Sumer Broota (`SUMB26-19`) now returns `is_coach_or_counselor() = true` and `is_my_assigned_student('BATO26-1') = true`, and reads **86** session logs where she previously read only her own 12, plus all 39 `coordinator_log_options`; she successfully inserted a renewal request for her assigned student and read it back; the *other* counsellor (`TESC26-18`, no assignment) was **blocked** from inserting one for the same student (`42501`, fails closed); and student Batu (`BATO26-1`), given a temporary published card for Sumer plus a decoy card for the unassigned counsellor, read **exactly two** profiles — his coach Rana's and his counsellor Sumer's — with the decoy correctly filtered out.

**Frontend:** a counsellor gets the **Performance Coach sidebar verbatim** — same tabs, same pages, same single log, with only the nav section header named after their own role — instead of the old three-item one; the CC-only roster page (`/teacher/cc-students`) is **deleted** and both roles share `/teacher/students` (the full `StudentsListView`, opening the full student detail view), with `myRosterStudentIds` reading both assignment tables so a PC-and-CC sees one combined list; **Renewal Requests moved out of the Performance Coaches group in both panels** and is now a standalone top-level tab named simply **Renewal Requests** (`/admin/renewal-requests`, `/teacher/renewal-requests`); new **`/admin/ccs/:id`** CC detail page (Details / Students / Profile) and new student **`/student/my-cc`** tab, which appears only for a student who actually has a live counsellor. See `README.md` §2.18/§3.

**Updated 2026-08-01, later (first Beyond Academic operational data — teacher David created, plus Batu Ozcelik's 4 sessions with him; no schema change).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260807000200_seed_david_and_batu_passion_project.sql` and `20260807000300_batu_passion_project_remaining_sessions.sql` (both data-only), verified live. Transcribed from the client's "David Session Log (2026)" sheet, which covers many students; Batu appears on 4 rows, all Beyond Academic / Passion Project. (The companion "2025" sheet contains no Batu rows at all — checked, not assumed.) These are the first Beyond Academic session logs in the system.

**David** had no `teachers` row, so he is created here: `DAVD26-20`, first-name-only with `last_name` repeated ("David David" — the column has been NOT NULL + non-blank since 2026-07-29, so it cannot be left empty), `david@ascendnow.info`, and placeholder country/phone for an admin to correct in the UI. The id came from `trg_generate_teacher_id`. **No login was created** — unlike the 2026-07-28 seed, which hand-built `auth.users` rows for its teachers — because a login is only useful once the real address is known, and `create-teacher-with-user` (which also emails credentials) must never be pointed at a placeholder address.

The **2026-05-31** row is a **Demo Lesson**, not a project session: its Session No column reads "INTRO" rather than a number and it is Batu's first session with David, so it takes the same shape as his other demo (`program_type_id` 4, `course_type_id` and `subject_id` NULL, deducts from nothing — see the correction in the entry below). It is **not** a duplicate of Sumer's 2026-05-31 College Counselling row: both teachers were on the same intro call and each logs their own time against their own program (David 0.75 hrs, Sumer 0.5 hrs), and neither touches a package. Its Video Link cell is empty — the sheet's "Processed on 01.06.26" month marker sits in that area, not a URL — so `video_link` is NULL.

The other three are **Beyond Academic / "Passion Projects"** (subject 444), `coordinator_teacher_id` Rana as on every other log of Batu's. They could not resolve automatically: Batu's All-In-One bundle has four unlocked Beyond Academic pools and "Passion Projects" matches none of their `pool_label`s, so `handle_session_log_package_lock()` flags `pool_ambiguous` and leaves `student_package_id` NULL for the PC to decide. **The two migrations are split precisely so exactly one session was pending at a time** — `notify-pool-issue` skips whenever another ambiguous, still-unassigned session already exists for the same (student, course_type, teacher, subject), so inserting all three at once would make *every* invocation find a sibling and skip, and nobody would be told at all. Applying `…000200`, then taking the pool decision, then `…000300` reproduces what would have happened had David logged the sessions one at a time through the UI. The decision was taken in the app: 2026-06-07 was assigned to the bundle's **"Primary Project"** pool, which wrote `session_log_pool_resolutions` id 1 for (BATO26-1, Beyond Academic, DAVD26-20, Passion Projects) → package 2; the two rows in `…000300` then resolved straight to that pool on insert via the trigger's sticky-resolution branch, with 0 ambiguous remaining.

Live counts for `BATO26-1` now: `session_logs` **86** (69 Academic + 12 College Counselling + 3 Beyond Academic + 2 Demo Lesson), `teachers` **16**. Pools: Academic 85.75/100, Primary Project **1.25/32** over 3 logs, College Counselling 9.25/40 over 12, and Secondary Project / Extra Hours / Career Exploration still untouched.

**Updated 2026-08-01 (first College Counselling operational data — Batu Ozcelik's All-In-One package and his 13 CC session logs; no schema change).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260807000000_seed_batu_ozcelik_cc_session_logs.sql` (data-only), verified live. Transcribed from the client's "Sumer Session Log (2026)" sheet — Sumer Broota's own counselling log, covering ~40 students; these are the 13 rows belonging to Batu, the one student in the system. Until now every session log in the database was Academic (program type 1); these are the first with `program_type_id`/`course_type_id` **3** (College Counselling) and the first written by a counsellor rather than a subject teacher.

The migration does two things in order, and the order is load-bearing. **First** it gives Batu an **All-In-One** package: that course type is a *bundle*, so it fans out into five `student_packages` rows grouped by `package_type_id` = All-In-One's own course_type id, exactly as `review-enrollment-payment` does on a confirmed enrollment — Beyond Academic "Primary Project" 32h, "Secondary Project" 24h, "Extra Hours" 16h, "Career Exploration" 10h, and College Counselling (unlabelled) 40h. The five rows and their hours are read from **`bundle_pool_settings`** rather than hardcoded, so they stay whatever an admin has set in Admin → Reports → Settings, and the hours arrive as `package_topups` rows so `on_package_topup_inserted()` accumulates `total_hours_purchased` (same path Batu's 100-hour Academic package was created through). **Then** it inserts the 13 logs, which `handle_session_log_package_lock()` resolves to the single unlocked College Counselling pool — **0 ambiguous, 0 fallback, 0 unlinked**. Run in the other order, each log would have been flagged `pool_fallback_used` against an ad-hoc zero-hour pool instead.

**Corrected same day** by `20260807000100_batu_demo_session_is_demo_lesson.sql`: the 2026-01-01 row's topic is literally "Demo Session" — the free intro call that happens *before* a student buys anything — so it is a **Demo Lesson** (`program_type_id` 4), not a College Counselling session, and must not bill against a package Batu did not own on that date. It now carries `course_type_id` **NULL** and `subject_id` **NULL**, which is exactly what `SessionLogFormView` produces for a demo: `course_type_id` is billing-only and independent of `program_types`, `PROGRAM_TYPE_TO_COURSE_TYPE_NAME` has no `demo_lesson` entry and no `course_types` row is named "Demo Lesson", and `showSubjectPicker` is false for a demo. That NULL course type is what makes a demo non-billable end to end — `handle_session_log_package_lock()` nulls `student_package_id` when it is NULL, and `useInvoices` skips any session without a course type, so demos never reach an invoice either. Duration stays 1 hr, teacher and coordinator unchanged. The College Counselling pool therefore holds **12** logs, not 13.

Live counts for `BATO26-1` now: `student_packages` **6** (the pre-existing standalone Academic 100h at `hours_used` 85.75, untouched, + the 5 All-In-One pools totalling 122h), `session_logs` **82** (69 Academic + 12 College Counselling + 1 Demo Lesson), College Counselling pool **9.25 / 40 hrs used**. All 13 carry `teacher_id` = `SUMB26-19` (Sumer Broota — she ran the sessions; it is her log) and `coordinator_teacher_id` = `RANW26-3` (Rana Walid Abdelaal), `curriculum_id` NULL, no no-shows, no flags, and no `fathom_summary` (the sheet carries only a topic, unlike the academic sheet). The 12 counselling rows carry subject `College Counselling` (id 447); the demo row carries none, per the correction above.

Three transcription decisions, all confirmed with the client and recorded in the migration header: (1) the sheet's Coordinator column is the student's **PC**, which is Rana throughout — its cell reads "Sumer" on 3 rows and is blank on the first, and that is **not** carried over, matching all 69 existing logs; (2) durations are **rounded to the nearest quarter hour**, because the sheet records Fathom's raw decimal call lengths (0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 1.1, 1.4) and `session_logs_session_duration_hrs_check` accepts only quarter-hour multiples — 7 of the 13 rows were affected and each carries its original figure in a trailing SQL comment; total logged is **10.25 hrs against 10.1 hrs** of actual call time; (3) the 2026-07-17 row has `video_link` **NULL**, since the sheet's Video Link cell there reads "WhatsApp (Network Issue)" rather than a Fathom URL — the note was not stored as though it were a link. The sheet spells Batu's surname four different ways (Ozenlick / Oznelick / Ozlenick / Ozlneick) and omits it entirely on two rows; the student is matched by id, so `students.last_name` (**Ozcelik**) is what the logs carry.

**Updated 2026-07-31 (bug fix: every College Counsellor was being given the wrong login role).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260806000300_sync_teacher_role_knows_about_cc.sql`, verified live. `sync_teacher_pc_role()` predated the CC role and only knew about `is_performance_coach`; its `else` branch forced `users.role = 'teacher'`, so the `AFTER INSERT` trigger fired immediately after `create-teacher-with-user` had correctly created the account as `college_counselor` and **overwrote it back to `teacher`**. Both CCs created so far had the wrong role.

Two visible symptoms, both reported: `/teacher/cc-students` is gated on `role = 'college_counselor'`, so a counsellor clicking "My Students" was bounced back to `/teacher`; and because the sidebar reads the login role first and the `teachers` flags only as a fallback, every navigation rendered the **plain-teacher sidebar** for the render before the teachers row arrived, then snapped to the CC one.

Replaced by **`sync_teacher_staff_role()`** with PC → CC → teacher precedence — the same rule `loginRoleForStaff()` in `src/utils/staffRole.ts` applies when the account is created, so the trigger can no longer contradict it. The trigger now fires on `UPDATE OF is_performance_coach, is_college_counselor` (it watched only the PC column, so flipping the CC flag on an existing teacher changed nothing). The old function was dropped. Existing counsellors were backfilled by a scoped `update` touching only rows that actually disagreed.

**Verified live:** both CCs now read `college_counselor` and all 13 other teacher/PC rows are unchanged; and all four transitions were exercised in a rolled-back transaction — flag CC → `college_counselor`, flag CC **and** PC → `performance_coach` (PC wins), unflag PC keeping CC → `college_counselor`, unflag both → `teacher`.

**Updated 2026-07-31 (new College Counsellor role — `is_college_counselor` flag, `cc_student_assignments`, and a `college_counselor` login role).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260806000000_college_counselor_role_enum.sql` + `20260806000100_cc_student_assignments.sql`, verified live. A CC is a `teachers` row flagged **`is_college_counselor`**, exactly the way a performance coach is one flagged `is_performance_coach`; students are assigned through the new **`cc_student_assignments`** table, managed by an admin at `/admin/cc-assignments`. A student who has a PC may *also* have a CC — the two assignment tables are independent, and having a PC remains the norm while a CC is optional.

The structural difference between the two roles is durability, and it is the whole reason CC assignments carry a status when PC assignments don't. A PC assignment is permanent for the life of the engagement, so its state lives on `students.status` (see the 2026-07-29 entry below). A CC engagement is a finite piece of work that ends while the student carries on with everything else, so **`cc_student_assignments.status`** (`active` / `completed`, deliberately **no** `paused`) sits on the assignment row. Completing sets `unassigned_at` and **never deletes the row** — session logs written during the engagement stay exactly where they are, and the closed row is what still attributes them to the counsellor who wrote them. Completing the *student* (`set_student_status(..., 'completed')`) now closes any live CC engagement too, alongside the PC one it already closed.

Two new functions: **`is_college_counselor()`** (keyed off the `teachers` flag rather than `users.role`, so a teacher who is both a PC and a CC — `users.role` can only carry one of the two — still gets CC access) and **`set_cc_assignment_status()`** (SECURITY DEFINER; admin or the assigned counsellor; complete/reopen). RLS on `cc_student_assignments` mirrors `pc_student_assignments` including its admin-only write rule: a counsellor cannot assign themselves a student. A CC's session-log access was, at the time, deliberately **narrower than a PC's**: they read only the logs they wrote themselves. (**Superseded 2026-08-03** — see the entry at the top of this file; a counsellor now reads session logs exactly as a coach does.) `20260806000100` originally gave them `CCs can read sessions for assigned students` (every session for an assigned student, by any teacher, mirroring the PC policy); that was **dropped again the same day** by `20260806000200_cc_reads_only_own_session_logs.sql` — a counsellor's business is their own counselling sessions, not the student's academic sessions with every other teacher. The existing `"Teachers can read their own session logs"` (`teacher_id = my_teacher_id()`) is now their only read, and logging a session needs no new policy either, since a counsellor writes as a teacher under the existing `teacher_id = my_teacher_id()` INSERT policy.

**Verified live by impersonation** (two rolled-back transactions, live data untouched — `cc_student_assignments` confirmed back to 0 rows afterwards): a counsellor was blocked from self-assigning (`42501`); an admin assigned successfully; a second *active* CC for the same student was rejected by `uq_active_cc_per_student`; the counsellor completed their own engagement (status `completed`, `unassigned_at` set) and an admin reopened it (`unassigned_at` back to NULL); completing the student closed both the CC and the PC assignment; and a session log the counsellor wrote for an assigned student was inserted, read back, and still readable to both the counsellor and an admin after the engagement completed, with the closed assignment row still present.

**Frontend:** new admin section **College Counsellors** (`/admin/ccs`, `/admin/cc-assignments`), a CC roster at `/teacher/cc-students`, and a CC sidebar limited to Session Logging + My Students + My Profile (**both superseded 2026-08-03** — that roster page is deleted and a counsellor now gets the Performance Coach sidebar verbatim). `create-teacher-with-user` redeployed (**v12**) to accept `is_college_counselor` and pick the login role accordingly — the welcome email it already sent now reads "your Ascend Now **CC** account" for a counsellor (PC/CC/teacher).

**Assignment notifications** (same date): new **`notify-assignment-change`** edge function (**v1**, `verify_jwt`) emails the *coach or counsellor* — never the student — when a student joins or leaves their roster, since assignment is admin-only and they'd otherwise only find out by checking their My Students page. Fired fire-and-forget from `usePcAssignments`/`useCcAssignments`/`setStudentStatus` on: assign, PC unassign, CC complete/reopen, CC delete (fired **before** the delete, while the row still exists to resolve), and student completion (which closes both assignments, so both people are told). It takes `{ role, event, assignment_id }` and resolves the student and staff member from the row itself rather than trusting a caller-supplied pair, so mail can't be addressed at an arbitrary person; a staff member with no email or a deactivated one is a silent skip, not an error. No schema change and no new grants were needed — `service_role` already had table privileges on `cc_student_assignments` from the default grants, verified live in `information_schema.role_table_grants`.

**Updated 2026-07-29 (Ruchi Steven's and Miriam Hanna's performance-coach profile cards filled in and published; data-only, no schema change).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260805000100_seed_ruchi_miriam_pc_profiles.sql`, verified live. Both had empty unpublished `pc_profiles` rows from `20260804000100`; the client supplied their finished cards, so each row now carries department `Management`, the "Hi there!" intro, 2 `achievements`, 4 `coach_responsibilities` timeline entries, education entries (Ruchi 1, Miriam 3) and contact details, with `is_published = true`. **3 of 5 coach profiles are now published** (Rana, Ruchi, Miriam); Michel Sherif and Ana Isabel Galvan remain empty and unpublished. Ruchi's two achievements are single-figure IB DP predictions — `score_before` is empty and `score_scale` stores the whole trailing string `"45 (Predicted)"`, which `PcProfileCard` renders as `44/45 (Predicted)` via its no-`score_before` branch. Institution logos again point at assets shipped in `public/` and served at the site root (`stanford.avif`, `lse.jpg` for Ruchi; `worldacademy.png`, `jis.png`, `nyu.png` for Miriam), for the same reason given in Rana's entry below. The same two deliberate gaps apply to both: `photo_url` is **NULL** (a headshot can only be uploaded to the public `pc-profiles` bucket through the UI, so the cards fall back to initials) and `educator_experience` is left **empty** because that section was removed from the feature — `PcProfileCard` never renders it and `PcProfileEditor` hardcodes `educator_experience: []` on every save. Note that Miriam's supplied card repeats Rana's two achievements and four responsibilities verbatim; that is what the client provided and it is stored as given rather than reworded.

**Updated 2026-07-29 (students now have an Active / On pause / Completed status, and completing one unassigns them).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260805000000_student_status_categories.sql`, verified live. `students` gains **`status`** (NOT NULL, default `'active'`, CHECK ∈ `{active, paused, completed}`, indexed) and **`status_changed_at`**. Both a performance coach and an admin can move a student between the three categories; the admin and coach student lists (`/admin/students`, `/teacher/students`, plus the assigned-students lists on `/admin/pc-assignments` and `/admin/pcs/:id`) group by it.

The status lives on `students`, **not** on `pc_student_assignments`, because marking a student `completed` also **closes their active assignment** — putting the state on the assignment would make it vanish at the exact moment it's set. Writes go through the new SECURITY DEFINER **`set_student_status()`** (see Functions) rather than a policy: RLS is row-level and cannot grant a coach a single column, and the completion path has to write `pc_student_assignments`, a table coaches deliberately cannot write. Consequence, by design: a coach cannot re-open a student they completed (they're no longer assigned to them) — an admin re-assigns from `/admin/pc-assignments`, which flips such a student back to `active`. **Verified live by impersonation:** an anonymous caller and an invalid status value were both rejected; an admin pause, a coach completion, and the resulting assignment closure all succeeded; and the coach was then correctly blocked from touching the student they had just completed (all inside a rolled-back transaction, leaving the live data untouched).

**Updated 2026-07-29 (a teacher's last name is now compulsory).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260804001000_teachers_last_name_required.sql`, verified live. `teachers.last_name` is now **`NOT NULL`** plus a **`teachers_last_name_not_blank`** CHECK (`btrim(last_name) <> ''`) — NOT NULL alone would still have accepted an empty string, which defeats the point.

Six teachers had no surname (`Anugya`, `Bharat`, `Ylenia`, `Magdaleina`, `Jesse`, `Sajjad`), created first-name-only by `20260728120000` / `20260804000200` because the client's session-log sheet only carried first names. Their real surnames are still unknown, and these names render on **student dashboards and invoice PDFs**, so rather than inventing plausible surnames — which would put a false name in front of students and paying customers — the backfill **repeats the first name** (`Anugya Anugya`), an explicit product decision. Replace them with the real surnames when known.

`create_teacher_profile()` was inserting a literal `NULL` last name, which the new constraint would reject outright, so it now mirrors the same repeat-the-first-name convention. (That function is separately broken — it writes to `teacher_subjects.subject_name`, a column that no longer exists — which is pre-existing and was deliberately left alone; this change only stops the NOT NULL adding a *second* failure mode.)

Enforced in the app too: the last-name field is now `required` on `/admin/teachers/new` + `/admin/teachers/:id/edit` (which is also the PC form), on the teacher's and coach's own profile pages (`TeacherProfilePage`/`PcProfilePage` — a self-edit could otherwise blank it and hit the constraint), and on the Add-admin form; `create-teacher-with-user` (**v8**) and `create-admin-with-user` (**v9**) both now reject a missing `last_name` with a 400 instead of letting it reach the database. `Teacher.last_name` is `string` (no longer `string | null`) in `src/types/database.ts`.

**Verified live:** all 13 teachers have a non-null last name (0 nulls); an attempted insert with a blank (`'   '`) surname is rejected by the CHECK and one with a missing surname by NOT NULL, both confirmed by an explicit test that would have raised if either were accepted, leaving the table at 13 rows. **Note:** the six backfilled teachers keep ids ending in `X` (`ANUX26-7`, `BHAX26-8`, `YLEX26-9`, `MAGX26-10`, `JESX26-11`, `SAJX26-12`) — that letter was derived when they genuinely had no surname. Ids are opaque and were deliberately **not** regenerated (it would mean another cascade across all 20 FK columns), so the `X` is now cosmetically stale rather than meaningful.

**Updated 2026-07-29 (staff ids converted to the same mnemonic text format as students — `teachers.id` and `admins.id` are no longer bigint).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260804000900_staff_mnemonic_ids.sql`, verified live. Every person in the system — student, teacher, performance coach, admin — is now identified the same way. Existing rows **keep their old numeric id as the sequence suffix** (teacher 3 → `RANW26-3`, admin 2 → `ARIS26-2`), exactly like `S1` → `BATO26-1`, so the old→new map is trivially unique and nothing changes position. New rows get their id from `trg_generate_teacher_id` / `trg_generate_admin_id` off the new `teachers_num_seq` / `admins_num_seq` (both `setval` to `max(id)+1` at conversion, and both granted explicit sequence `USAGE` per `db/README.md`'s "one rule to never forget", since a trigger-populated text PK is not an identity column). `admins` has no `first_name`/`last_name` of its own, so its code derives from the linked `users.full_name`.

This touched far more than two columns, so the migration was written to derive its own work rather than hand-list it: **20 FK columns across 18 tables** and **all 145 `public` RLS policies** were captured from the catalog, dropped, and rebuilt verbatim from the captured definitions (a policy-count assertion fails the whole migration if any doesn't come back). `my_teacher_id()` (bigint → **text**) and `create_teacher_profile()` (returns bigint → **text**) were dropped before the type change and recreated after — as SQL/plpgsql functions over `teachers.id` they'd otherwise be left returning the wrong type at runtime — and `sync_coordinator_log_for_student()`'s `v_pc_id bigint` declaration was retyped in place (guarded by an assertion that the declaration still exists, rather than restating the whole body). Storage-schema policies were checked first and reference `teachers.user_id`, never `.id`, so they neither blocked the change nor needed rebuilding.

**Verified live after applying:** policies **145 → 145**, teacher FKs **20 → 20**, and every row count identical to the pre-migration snapshot (`teachers` 13, `admins` 2, `session_logs` 69 — with all 69 still carrying both `teacher_id` and `coordinator_teacher_id` — `teacher_subjects` 9, `pc_profiles` 5, `pc_student_assignments` 1); a loop over all 20 FK columns found **0 orphaned references**; `my_teacher_id()` returns `text`; and RLS was re-tested by impersonation both ways — PC Rana (`RANW26-3`) still sees exactly her 1 assigned student and 1 coordinator log, while a plain non-PC teacher (`ANUX26-7`) still sees **0** coordinator logs (fails closed). The insert trigger was exercised (`Priya Nair` → `PRIN26-15`, continuing the sequence) and the test row deleted. `get_advisors(security)` reports **no ERROR-level findings** (only the pre-existing WARN categories). The first apply attempt failed on `cannot use subquery in transform expression` for the `admins` column and rolled back with zero changes — confirmed by re-reading the counts before retrying with a helper-function lookup, the same indirection already used for teachers.

**Frontend converted in the same change** (the type is load-bearing across the app): `Teacher.id` and every `*_teacher_id` field became `string`, teacher-keyed `Map`/`Set` types were rekeyed, and `Number(...)` coercions on teacher ids removed — ~60 files. "Order by id" sorts now compare the trailing sequence via the new `src/utils/entityId.ts` `idSeqNumber()` helper, because plain string sorting would order by the name prefix and silently turn those lists into name sorts. `tsc -b` and the test suite pass, with lint output unchanged from baseline.

**Updated 2026-07-29 (admins now store phone number + country; 2 new columns).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260804000800_admins_phone_country.sql`, verified live via `information_schema`. `admins` previously held no contact/location info at all (just `id`/`user_id`/`created_at`/`updated_at`) — it now has nullable **`phone_number`** and **`country`**, the same two optional fields `teachers` already carries for teachers and performance coaches. Collected on the inline "Add admin" form on `/admin/admins`, reusing the teacher/PC form's `SelectInput` country picker + `PhoneInput` dial-code pair (so a stored phone has the same `+<dial> <number>` shape, and picking a country auto-selects its dial code), and written by **`create-admin-with-user`** (redeployed **v8**), which now accepts `country`/`phone_number` and passes them through on the `admins` insert. Both columns are also surfaced as new Country/Phone columns on the admins table. No RLS change needed — the single existing `"Admins can do everything on admins"` policy (`ALL` / `is_admin()`) already covers the page's direct read, confirmed live in `pg_policies`. Nothing back-fills these, so admins created before this (including the bootstrap `admin@123`) read NULL / "—" until edited — though as of 2026-08-04 an admin is made to fill both in at their own next login by `AdminCompleteProfileGate` (see the `admins` table section below). Note the page merges these two fields onto its user rows with a separate `admins` query keyed by `user_id`, since `useUsers()` reads only the `users` table.

**Updated 2026-07-29, even later still (Batu Ozcelik's existing student id renamed from S1 to the new mnemonic format; no schema change).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260804000700_rename_batu_ozcelik_student_id.sql`. Now that new students get a mnemonic id (previous entry), the one real student already in the system was moved to match: `S1` → `BATO26-1`, reusing his original sequence number (1) rather than consuming a fresh `nextval()` — he was already sequence value 1, this only changes the rendering. Renamed the PK on `students` plus every one of the 14 FK columns that reference it, in one transaction under `session_replication_role = replica` (same technique as `20260803000000_purge_teachers_students_sessions.sql`, needed because those FKs are `NOT DEFERRABLE` and would otherwise fail mid-update regardless of which order parent/children are touched): `content_uploads`, `coordinator_logs`, `enrollment_requests`, `generated_papers`, `invoices`, `monthly_report_student_stats`, `monthly_report_student_subject_stats`, `package_renewal_requests`, `pc_student_assignments`, `session_log_pool_resolutions`, `session_logs`, `student_packages`, `subject_notes`, `submissions` — this list was pulled live from `information_schema` rather than assumed, and is longer than what earlier entries in this doc mention from memory (e.g. it includes `content_uploads`/`generated_papers`/`submissions`/`package_renewal_requests`/`session_log_pool_resolutions`, which hadn't been enumerated here before). Verified live post-rename: zero rows anywhere still reference `'S1'`, and `BATO26-1` shows the same row counts as before the rename (`students` 1, `session_logs` 69, `student_packages` 1, `pc_student_assignments` 1) — no orphans, no data loss. Also checked `storage.objects` for any `'S1/'`-prefixed path across every bucket before renaming: none existed, so no storage files needed moving alongside it. Historical entries elsewhere in this doc that refer to him as `S1` describe what was true at the time and were deliberately left as-is rather than rewritten.

**Updated 2026-07-29, later still (student id format changed from a bare sequence to a mnemonic code, then unpadded same day; no new tables).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260804000500_student_id_mnemonic_format.sql` and a same-day follow-up, `20260804000600_student_id_sequence_no_padding.sql`, both verified live via `execute_sql`. `students.id` is no longer a plain column `DEFAULT` of `'S' || nextval('students_num_seq')` — a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger (`trg_generate_student_id` / `generate_student_id()`) builds it from the student's own name instead, since the format needs `first_name`/`last_name` from the row being inserted: first 3 letters of `first_name` + first letter of `last_name` (letters only, uppercased, padded/fallback `X` when too short) + 2-digit enrollment year + `-` + `nextval('students_num_seq')` (unpadded as of the follow-up — the first version zero-padded to 4 digits, dropped same day since a fixed width reads like an artificial cap even though it never actually truncates), e.g. Batu Ozcelik enrolling in 2026 → `BATO26-1`. The sequence is unchanged and still the actual uniqueness guarantee; the mnemonic prefix is cosmetic on top of it, and the trigger only fires when `id` isn't already supplied on insert. Verified live with three throwaway inserts, each deleted immediately after confirming the shape: `Diya Kapoor` → `DIYK26-0002` and short-name+apostrophe case `Al O'Brien` → `ALXO26-0003` (both under the padded version), then `Priya Sharma` → `PRIS26-4` (after unpadding). See the `students` table section below for full detail.

**Updated 2026-07-29, later (performance-coach roster created + Batu Ozcelik data restored after the purge; the missing `trg_create_user_profile` trigger fixed).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260804000000_restore_create_user_profile_trigger.sql`, `20260804000100_seed_performance_coaches.sql`, `20260804000200_reseed_batu_ozcelik_student_and_teachers.sql` and `20260804000300_reseed_batu_ozcelik_session_logs.sql`, all verified live. This resolves **both** findings recorded in the entry below.

- **`trg_create_user_profile` restored.** Re-created exactly as the baseline declares it, and it works: every `auth.users` insert in the three seeds that follow produced its `public.users` row automatically. The earlier diagnosis that the `auth.users` owner (`supabase_auth_admin`) blocks migrations running as `postgres` did **not** hold — `CREATE TRIGGER` succeeded through the normal `apply_migration` path. The likelier cause of its absence is that this project (ref `rpavogsdladesmghwodg`, created 2026-07-28) was provisioned fresh: Supabase provisions the `auth` schema itself, so a custom trigger attached to `auth.users` is not carried across. Every other `public` trigger had survived — all 16 checked on `users`/`teachers`/`students`/`session_logs`/`package_topups`/`admins`/`pc_profiles`/`student_packages` were present and enabled. **`create-admin-with-user` and `create-teacher-with-user` therefore work again**; neither was re-tested end to end over HTTP (the project host is blocked by egress policy), but the trigger they depend on is confirmed firing.
- **Row counts re-verified.** The claim below that the catalogue was empty is **wrong/outdated**: the catalogue is present — `subjects` **449**, `curricula` **6**, `curriculum_groups` **33**, `course_types` **5**, `program_types` **8**. `style_templates` was genuinely **0** (the Homework Generator's seeded templates did not survive) — **fixed 2026-08-04**: `20260804000000_reseed_style_templates.sql` restored the 5 generic question types the builder offers, which had left the "Question type" dropdown empty and generation impossible. See that table's `###` section for what was and wasn't restored, and why.
- **Performance coaches (5).** Supplied directly by the client and previously created through the admin UI, so no migration could restore them after the purge deleted every teacher: **Michel Sherif**, **Rana Walid Abdelaal**, **Miriam Hanna**, **Ana Isabel Galvan**, **Ruchi Steven** — all `<first.last>@ascendnow.info`, `is_performance_coach = true`, `users.role = 'performance_coach'` (set by `trg_sync_teacher_pc_role`), each with an empty unpublished `pc_profiles` row (`is_published = false`) so the coach writes their own card at first login. **Rana's card was subsequently filled in and published** from copy supplied by the client (`20260804000400_seed_rana_walid_pc_profile.sql`): department `Management`, the "Hi there!" intro, 2 `achievements` (Ariel F, MYP 4 Math 3→6 /8, World Academy; Tobias B, AP Calculus BC 2→5 /5, JIS · NYU) — the institution logos point at assets already shipped in `public/` (`worldacademy.png`, `jis.png`, `nyu.png`), served at the site root; `PcProfileCard` renders them via a plain `<img src>`, so a root-relative path resolves against the same origin and keeps the logos versioned with the repo rather than depending on a `pc-profiles` bucket upload, 4 `coach_responsibilities` timeline entries, 3 `education` entries, and contact details — `is_published = true`, so her assigned student (Batu, `S1`) now sees it on the Overview tab. Two deliberate gaps: `photo_url` is **NULL** (the headshot must be uploaded to the public `pc-profiles` bucket through the UI — it can't be created from SQL; the card falls back to her initials), and `educator_experience` is left **empty** even though the supplied card has an "Educator Experience" section, because that section was removed from the feature — `PcProfileCard` never renders the field and `PcProfileEditor` hardcodes `educator_experience: []` on every save, so any value stored there would render nowhere and be wiped on her first save. Restoring that section needs a UI change first. Note also that `score_scale` stores the bare number (`"8"`), since the card renders it as `/{score_scale}`. **`country` is inferred from each phone number's dialling code** (+20 → Egypt ×3, +52 → Mexico, +91 → India) — the client gave names/emails/phones only, so that one field is worth confirming in the admin UI.
- **Batu Ozcelik data replayed.** `20260728120000` / `20260728120100` were already recorded in the migration history but their rows had been deleted by the purge, so the two `2026080400020x` migrations replay them (past migrations are never edited). Restored to exactly the documented pre-purge shape: `students` **1** (`S1`), `teachers` **11** (5 coaches + the 6 subject teachers), `teacher_subjects` **8**, `pc_student_assignments` **1** (Rana), `student_packages` **1** (100 hrs, `hours_used` **85.75**), `session_logs` **69** (2025-12-30 → 2026-05-10; Anugya/Integrated Humanities 16, Bharat/Extended Mathematics 26, Ylenia/Spanish 15, Magdaleina/Personal Project 2 + English 1, Jesse/Integrated Sciences 2, Sajjad/Integrated Sciences 6 + Chemistry 1), 0 rows missing a coordinator, 0 unlinked from the package. The session-log statement was verified **byte-identical** to the original migration's by comparing `md5(string_agg(...))` over `video_link`, `topic` and `fathom_summary` against the on-disk file (matching once both sides sort under `collate "C"` — the first comparison differed only because Postgres's default collation orders the URLs differently from a codepoint sort, not because of any data difference).
- **Live counts now:** `users`/`auth.users` **13** (1 admin + 5 coaches + 6 teachers + 1 student), `admins` **1**, `teachers` **11**, `pc_profiles` **5**, `students` **1**, `session_logs` **69**.
- **New edge function `send-teacher-credentials` (deployed 2026-07-29).** `create-teacher-with-user` emails credentials only as a side effect of *creating* the account, so there was no way to send that email to someone already in the system — which is every staff member seeded by a migration, since the direct `auth.users` insert path deliberately sends none. This function takes `{ email, password }`, looks the teacher up, and sends the byte-identical welcome email; admin-only auth mirrors `create-teacher-with-user`, and the SMTP send is **awaited** (not backgrounded) so the caller learns whether the mail server accepted it. The password cannot be read back from `auth.users` (bcrypt), so the caller supplies it — verify first with `select encrypted_password = crypt('<pw>', encrypted_password) from auth.users where email = '<email>'`. Used to send Ruchi Steven her PC account email (HTTP 200, `sent_to: ruchi.steven@ascendnow.info`). **No UI yet** — there is no button on `/admin/teachers`; it currently has to be invoked directly.
- **`pg_net` was enabled transiently and then dropped.** Egress from the Claude session to the project host is blocked by proxy policy, so the function was triggered from inside the database via `pg_net`. It was dropped again afterwards (`drop extension … cascade`), which both restores the pre-send schema and clears the `net._http_response` rows that held the admin access/refresh tokens in plaintext. Net schema change: **none**.
- **Known cosmetic gap:** `teachers.id` starts at **2**, not 1 — the first insert attempt failed on the FK described above and burned sequence value 1 (sequences don't roll back). Harmless; ids are opaque.
- **Migration-version drift (important for anyone running `supabase db push`).** The Supabase MCP `apply_migration` tool records a version stamped with **the time it ran**, not the filename's prefix. These four were therefore recorded as `20260729100006` / `20260729100101` / `20260729100224` / `20260729100820` (and the admin seed as `20260729092450`), while the files are named `20260804…`. The filenames were deliberately **kept** in the `20260804…` range so that a from-scratch replay orders them *after* the `20260803…` purge — renaming them to match the recorded versions would sort them *before* it, and the purge would then delete everything they seed. The trade-off is that `db push` will not recognise these files as applied, so **`20260804000300` carries a `NOT EXISTS` guard on `video_link`** (a natural key: 69 rows / 69 distinct links) making a re-push a no-op; verified by re-running a present row through the guard — 0 inserted, count still 69. The other three were already idempotent.
- **Pre-existing duplicate migration version (not introduced here, not fixed here):** `20260801000000_stored_package_hours_used.sql` and `20260801000000_subject_notes.sql` share one version prefix. The CLI keys migrations by that prefix, so this is worth resolving separately.

**Updated 2026-07-29 (bootstrap admin re-seeded; two live-drift findings recorded — no schema change).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260804000000_seed_initial_admin.sql` (data-only, idempotent), verified live. The project had **zero** users, so nobody could sign in — and the app cannot bootstrap itself out of that, because `/admin/admins` → `create-admin-with-user` requires an already-authenticated admin caller. One admin was created: username **`admin@123`** (login is by username — see `get_email_for_username`), auth email `admin@ascendnow.com`, `users.role = 'admin'`, `is_active = true`, plus its `admins` marker row (id 1) and an `auth.identities` row. Password is bcrypt via `crypt()/gen_salt('bf')` and is committed in plaintext in the migration, matching the existing teacher/student seeds — it is a bootstrap credential and should be changed from the profile page after first sign-in. Verified by reading back the row set and confirming `crypt()` matches the stored hash and that `get_email_for_username('admin@123')` resolves to the Auth email; an end-to-end HTTP sign-in could not be run from this environment (the project host is blocked by egress policy).

Two things found while doing it, both **pre-existing** and neither caused by this change:

1. **`trg_create_user_profile` does not exist on the live database.** The baseline declares `CREATE TRIGGER trg_create_user_profile AFTER INSERT ON auth.users …`, but `auth.users` is owned by `supabase_auth_admin` while migrations run as `postgres`, so that statement never took effect here — every *other* baseline trigger (`trg_prevent_privilege_escalation`, `trg_touch_users`, `trg_touch_admins`, `trg_sync_teacher_pc_role`) is present. **Consequence: creating an Auth user no longer creates its `public.users` row.** That silently breaks `create-admin-with-user` and `create-teacher-with-user`, which both rely on the trigger for the profile row (each would leave an Auth login with no profile, and `create-admin-with-user` would then fail its `admins` insert and roll the Auth user back). The admin seed above therefore inserts `public.users` explicitly rather than relying on the trigger. ~~**Not fixed here**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-07-29 (see the entry above)**: the trigger was restored via `20260804000000_restore_create_user_profile_trigger.sql` and is confirmed firing. The ownership explanation given here also turned out to be wrong — `CREATE TRIGGER` on `auth.users` succeeded through the normal migration path.
2. **This doc's row counts are stale against this project.** The header below records ref `uqyuczvckqxgtilpzarh`; the live project is **`rpavogsdladesmghwodg`** (created 2026-07-28). It holds the full schema but almost no data — including the catalogue the 2026-07-27 purge entry says was kept: `subjects` **0**, `curricula` **0**, `curriculum_groups` **0**, `teachers` **0**, `students` **0**, `session_logs` **0**, `teacher_subjects` **0**, `student_packages` **0**; `users`/`auth.users`/`admins` are **1/1/1** (the admin above). The per-table counts in "Tables" below are the old project's pre-purge snapshot and should not be trusted until re-verified. **Partly RESOLVED 2026-07-29 (see the entry above)**: the catalogue counts asserted here are **incorrect** — `subjects`/`curricula`/`curriculum_groups` are **449/6/33**, not 0. Current verified counts for the operational tables are in that entry; the per-table numbers further down this doc remain the old project's snapshot.

**Updated 2026-07-27 (FULL DATA PURGE — all teachers, students & session logs removed; no schema change).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260803000000_purge_teachers_students_sessions.sql` (data-only), verified live. The operational data was wiped back to a clean slate: **all 149 `teachers` (incl. all 32 performance coaches → 0), all 3 `students` → 0, all 66 `session_logs` → 0**, and every dependent table emptied — `pc_student_assignments`, `pc_profiles`, `coordinator_logs` + `coordinator_log_subjects` + `coordinator_log_package_statuses`, `student_packages`, `package_topups`, `invoices` + `invoice_line_items` + `invoice_packages` (incl. the 2 locked invoices), `monthly_reports` + all 5 `monthly_report_*_stats`, `enrollment_requests` + `enrollment_request_packages` + `enrollment_request_payment_proofs`, `package_renewal_requests`, `subject_notes`, `zoom_invoices`, `teacher_subjects`, `session_log_pool_resolutions`, and the Homework Generator content (`generated_papers`, `content_uploads`, `submissions`, `grades`, `question_bank`, `session_insights`). Every non-admin `public.users` (152 rows) **and** matching `auth.users` login was deleted — only the **2 admins** remain (`auth.users` and `public.users` both = 2). Ran with `session_replication_role = replica` to bypass the locked-invoice / locked-monthly-report / locked-month-session-log delete guards and to sidestep FK ordering. Catalogue references to now-deleted rows were nulled so the catalogue survives intact: `subjects`/`curricula`.`added_by_teacher_id` and `.acknowledged_by_user_id` (the latter two are FKs to `auth.users`). **Kept, untouched:** `subjects` (454), `curricula` (12), `curriculum_groups` (40), `subject_categories` (7), `program_types` (10), `course_types` (7), `style_templates` (67), `coordinator_log_options` (39), and the single-row settings tables (`no_show_settings`, `session_duration_settings`, `bundle_pool_settings`). All storage objects were also cleared from the `homework-answers`, `homework-content`, `payment-proofs`, `pc-profiles`, `report-cards`, `subject-notes`, `session-invoices`, `session-summaries`, and `avatars` buckets (direct `storage.objects` delete via `session_replication_role = replica` to bypass `storage.protect_delete()`; the underlying S3 blobs are orphaned but unreachable once the metadata rows are gone). No table/column/policy/function/trigger changed — the row counts throughout this doc below are the pre-purge snapshot; treat this entry as authoritative for what's now live (only admins + catalogue + settings). **ID numbering was also reset** (`supabase/migrations/20260803000100_reset_id_sequences_after_purge.sql`): every ID sequence owned by an emptied table was `RESTART WITH 1`, plus the free-standing `students_num_seq` (so the next student code is **S1**) and the dead `parents_num_seq` — so the next teacher is id `1`, the next session log id `1`, etc. The kept catalogue/settings sequences (`subjects`, `curricula`, `curriculum_groups`, `subject_categories`, `course_types`, `program_types`, `style_templates`, `coordinator_log_options`, `bundle_pool_settings`, `admins`) were deliberately **not** reset, since those tables still hold rows. See `README.md` §2.14.

**Updated 2026-07-18, later (Performance Coach visual profile — new coach → student profile feature, 1 new table + 1 new bucket; 46 tables now).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260802000000_pc_profiles.sql`, verified live and RLS end-to-end tested by impersonation. A performance coach fills in and maintains a Canva-style **visual profile** (department badge, "about me" intro, performing-achievements cards, educator experience, coach responsibilities at Ascend Now, education, contact, photo) from the new `/teacher/pc-profile` page (§3.4). Each of the coach's assigned students sees it at the top of their Overview tab ("Your Performance Coach", §3.7). It is **not** a mandatory first-login gate — the coach fills it in whenever ready; saving flips `is_published` so assigned students can then see it. New table **`pc_profiles`** (one row per coach, `teacher_id` unique; the repeatable sections — `achievements`/`educator_experience`/`coach_responsibilities`/`education` — are jsonb; see its own `###` section below) and new **public** `pc-profiles` storage bucket for the photo (see Storage Buckets). RLS: `is_admin()` → `ALL`; the owning coach → `ALL` scoped to `teacher_id = my_teacher_id()`; any staff member (a `teachers` row) → `SELECT` on all; a student → `SELECT` only on the profile of a coach with an **active** `pc_student_assignments` row for them. Verified live by impersonation: the coach could write their own row but was RLS-blocked from writing another teacher's, and an assigned student read **only** their coach's row (a decoy profile for another teacher was correctly filtered out). Explicit `GRANT`s (table + identity-sequence `USAGE`) to `authenticated`/`service_role`; `touch_pc_profiles_updated_at` reuses `touch_updated_at()`. No edge function — direct table + storage writes. See `README.md` §2.13/§3.4/§3.7.

**Updated 2026-07-18 (Reports — publish-to-student gate; 2 new `invoices` columns, student read policy tightened).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260801000400_invoice_publish_to_student.sql`, verified live. Reports (the `invoices` table) are now **draft by default and hidden from the student** until an admin/PC explicitly publishes them. New columns **`invoices.published_to_student_at`** (timestamptz, null = draft) and **`invoices.published_by_user_id`** (uuid → `users.id`). The student read policy `student_parent_read_invoices` was tightened from `can_view_student(student_id)` to `can_view_student(student_id) AND published_to_student_at IS NOT NULL` (live-verified in `pg_policies`), so a student only ever reads reports published to them — the `admin_all_invoices` and `pc_manage_assigned_invoices` `ALL` policies are unchanged, so staff still see every report. Set/cleared from the Reports tab on `/admin/students/:id` + `/teacher/students/:id` via the new **"Publish to student" / "Unpublish from student"** row action (`useInvoices.setInvoicePublished`). Independent of the lock lifecycle (`status` draft/sent/locked) — a report can be published whether or not it's locked. See `README.md` §3.2/§3.7.

**Updated 2026-07-17, later still (Zoom invoices are now DB-enforced as teacher-only — coaches excluded).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260801000200_zoom_invoices_teachers_only.sql`. The `zoom_invoices` INSERT and replace-`UPDATE` policies previously checked only `teacher_id = my_teacher_id()`; because a performance coach is also a `teachers` row, `my_teacher_id()` returned their id and let them create/replace a Zoom invoice. Both write policies now add `AND NOT EXISTS (select 1 from teachers t where t.id = teacher_id and t.is_performance_coach)`, so coaches are blocked at the DB layer (verified live in `pg_policies`). SELECT is unchanged (one pre-existing coach-owned row remains and is harmless). The `/admin/zoom-invoices` counts already excluded coaches; `/teacher/invoices` now also guards the page for a coach who reaches it directly (the sidebar already hid the link). No schema/table change.

**Updated 2026-07-17, later (Teacher deactivation now actually disables the login; backfilled 2 stale rows — no schema change).** Applied via `supabase/migrations/20260801000100_sync_inactive_teacher_users.sql`. Root cause: `prevent_self_privilege_escalation()` (BEFORE UPDATE ON `users`) only allowed `is_active`/`role` changes when `auth.uid()` was an admin, but the `deactivate-teacher-with-user` edge function runs under the **service role** (`auth.uid()` NULL), so its `users.update({ is_active:false })` was silently rejected — deactivating a teacher flipped `teachers.is_active` but left `users.is_active = true`, so those accounts still showed as **Active** on `/admin/users` and the Inactive status filter matched nothing. Fix: the trigger now returns early (allows the change) when `auth.uid()` IS NULL — a trusted backend/service-role context, which `users` RLS already guarantees can't be a non-admin self-edit. The migration also backfilled the two teachers deactivated before the fix (`ahmed.almansoori`, `radhikarora23`) to `users.is_active = false`; live-verified 0 inactive users → 2 after.
